Tests that target a vector\n    *other* than the allowlist need a permissive config so the failure\n    that surfaces is the vector under test (not a coincidental\n    allowlist miss).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    return EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(scratch_root,))\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Attack vector 1 — scratch root is a symlink to a non-allowlisted target\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestVectorScratchSymlinkToHome:\n    \"\"\"Vector 1 (``scratch-is-symlink-to-HOME``).\n\n    The catastrophic case from the roadmap risk register (R2): a\n    scratch root that *looks* like an allowed prefix but is actually a\n    symlink whose resolved target lies outside the allowlist — the\n    real ``$HOME``/``.claude`` being the worst-case target. The\n    AC12 allowlist check inside :func:`containment_guard` calls\n    :func:`resolve_scratch_root`, which resolves with\n    ``strict=False`` so any symlink component collapses before the\n    membership test runs. The expected failure surface is\n    :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` with\n    ``check=\"scratch_root_allowlist\"`` and ``__cause__`` chained to\n    :class:`ScratchRootViolation`.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def test_setup_rejects_scratch_root_symlinked_outside_allowlist(\n        self, tmp_path: Path\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Build a scratch root whose textual path is allowlist-friendly\n        but whose symlink target points at a stand-in for the real\n        ``$HOME`` (a directory deliberately outside the allowlist).\n\n        Calling :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` MUST raise\n        :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` with\n        ``check=\"scratch_root_allowlist\"`` and a chained\n        :class:`ScratchRootViolation` cause — proving the\n        symlink-resolved allowlist check is the layer that catches the\n        attack rather than any lexical prefix comparison.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        # Stand-in for the real ``$HOME``/``.claude`` an attacker would\n        # try to escape into. Deliberately *not* in the allowlist.\n        fake_real_home = tmp_path / \"FAKE_HOME_DOT_CLAUDE\"\n        fake_real_home.mkdir()\n        (fake_real_home / \"settings.json\").write_text(\"{}\")  # plausible target\n\n        # The \"legitimate-looking\" scratch root the caller passes is a\n        # symlink to the fake real HOME. Its lexical parent path is\n        # under tmp_path, but ``Path.resolve(strict=False)`` will\n        # collapse it to ``fake_real_home``.\n        scratch_link = tmp_path / \"looks-like-eval-runs\"\n        scratch_link.symlink_to(fake_real_home)\n\n        # Real allowlisted directory the operator *intended* — note it\n        # is a different, real directory; the symlink target is NOT\n        # this path.\n        allowed_dir = tmp_path / \"really-allowed\"\n        allowed_dir.mkdir()\n        config = EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(allowed_dir,))\n\n        iso = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=\"E1\",\n            home_root=scratch_link,\n            session_id=\"sess-vector-1\",\n        )\n\n        with pytest.raises(HomeContainmentViolation) as exc_info:\n            iso.setup(config=config)\n\n        assert exc_info.value.check == \"scratch_root_allowlist\"\n        assert isinstance(exc_info.value.__cause__, ScratchRootViolation)\n        # The exception payload preserves the verbatim (un-resolved)\n        # scratch root so forensics can show the operator the symlink\n        # they were tricked into supplying.\n        assert exc_info.value.scratch_root == scratch_link\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Attack vector 2 — scratch root outside the AC12 allowlist\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestVectorScratchOutsideAllowlist:\n    \"\"\"Vector 2 (``scratch-outside-allowlist``).\n\n    The scratch root is a real, non-symlinked directory whose path is\n    not present in :attr:`EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`. This is\n    the \"policy\" failure mode that catches a configuration mistake or\n    a malicious ``--scratch-root`` flag value pointing at an\n    unintended location.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def test_setup_rejects_scratch_root_not_in_allowlist(\n        self, tmp_path: Path\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"A real scratch directory that is not listed in the config's\n        ``allowed_scratch_roots`` must fail with ``check=\"scratch_root_allowlist\"``.\"\"\"\n\n        # Real directory, not symlinked anywhere.\n        scratch = tmp_path / \"off-policy-scratch\"\n        scratch.mkdir()\n\n        # Allowlist contains a *different* directory, mirroring a real\n        # production config (e.g. only ``/tmp/eval-runs`` is permitted).\n        allowed_only = tmp_path / \"only-allowed-policy\"\n        allowed_only.mkdir()\n        config = EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(allowed_only,))\n\n        iso = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=\"E1\",\n            home_root=scratch,\n            session_id=\"sess-vector-2\",\n        )\n\n        with pytest.raises(HomeContainmentViolation) as exc_info:\n            iso.setup(config=config)\n\n        assert exc_info.value.check == \"scratch_root_allowlist\"\n        assert isinstance(exc_info.value.__cause__, ScratchRootViolation)\n        assert exc_info.value.scratch_root == scratch\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Attack vector 3 — eval_id mutation after construction\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestVectorEvalIdMutationPostConstruction:\n    \"\"\"Vector 3 (``eval_id-mutation-post-construction``).\n\n    :class:`HomeIsolation` is a ``@dataclass(frozen=True)``; ordinary\n    assignment raises :class:`dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError`. However\n    a determined caller can still bypass the freeze via\n    ``object.__setattr__`` (the same mechanism the class itself uses\n    for the private ``_home_path`` slot). The defense-in-depth contract\n    requires that even an instance whose ``eval_id`` was force-mutated\n    *after* ``__post_init__`` ran still fails inside\n    :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` — the guard re-runs\n    :func:`validate_eval_id` so post-construction tampering cannot\n    sneak an unsafe id into ``tempfile.mkdtemp``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    @pytest.mark.parametrize(\n        \"tampered_eval_id\",\n        [\n            \"../escape\",  # path traversal\n            \"/etc/passwd\",  # absolute path injection\n            \"E1/x\",  # path separator smuggled in\n            \"..\",  # parent-directory token\n            \"9bad\",  # FR-SCH2 leading-digit reject\n            \"\",  # empty string\n            \"with spaces\",\n            \"E1\\nE2\",  # newline injection\n        ],\n    )\n    def test_setup_rejects_post_construction_eval_id_mutation(\n        self,\n        scratch_root: Path,\n        permissive_config: EvalConfig,\n        tampered_eval_id: str,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Construct ``HomeIsolation`` with a valid id (passes\n        ``__post_init__``), then force-mutate ``eval_id`` to a FR-SCH2-\n        rejected value via ``object.__setattr__`` and call\n        :meth:`setup`. The containment guard's eval_id check must\n        surface a :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` with\n        ``check=\"eval_id\"`` and :class:`InvalidEvalId` chained as\n        ``__cause__``.\"\"\"\n\n        iso = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=\"E1\",  # safe at construction\n            home_root=scratch_root,\n            session_id=\"sess-vector-3\",\n        )\n\n        # Bypass the dataclass freeze the same way an attacker (or an\n        # accidental ``__setattr__`` shim) would.\n        object.__setattr__(iso, \"eval_id\", tampered_eval_id)\n\n        with pytest.raises(HomeContainmentViolation) as exc_info:\n            iso.setup(config=permissive_config)\n\n        assert exc_info.value.check == \"eval_id\"\n        assert isinstance(exc_info.value.__cause__, InvalidEvalId)\n        assert exc_info.value.eval_id == tampered_eval_id\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Attack vector 4 — loader-bypass: caller skips SuiteLoader entirely\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestVectorLoaderBypass:\n    \"\"\"Vector 4 (``loader-bypass``).\n\n    The FR-SCH2 regex enforcement lives in\n    :func:`validate_eval_id` and is applied by\n    :class:`SuiteLoader` on every parsed-and-expanded eval id (T01.05\n    + T01.07). NFR-SEC2 mandates that a caller skipping the loader —\n    for example a programmatic test, a future REPL entry point, or an\n    accidentally instantiated ``HomeIsolation`` in an integration\n    fixture — STILL fails closed. Two layers enforce this:\n\n    1. ``HomeIsolation.__post_init__`` re-runs\n       :func:`validate_eval_id` at construction.\n    2. :func:`containment_guard` re-runs\n       :func:`validate_eval_id` inside :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`.\n\n    The first layer raises :class:`InvalidEvalId` directly because\n    ``__post_init__`` runs before any guard-wrapping; the second is\n    proven separately by the post-construction mutation vector above.\n    Acceptance criteria require this test to prove that *constructing*\n    ``HomeIsolation`` without going through :class:`SuiteLoader` still\n    fails containment — so we assert the constructor-time defense\n    here. :class:`InvalidEvalId` is the canonical loader-bypass\n    failure surface; in production code the CLI boundary maps it to\n    :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE` (= 2). This pins the same\n    error class :func:`containment_guard` would have chained as\n    ``__cause__`` if the constructor's guard had been removed —\n    keeping the bypass closure consistent across both layers.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    @pytest.mark.parametrize(\n        \"loader_rejected_eval_id\",\n        [\n            \"../escape\",\n            \"/absolute/path\",\n            \"E1/with/sep\",\n            \"9bad\",\n            \"{{template}}\",\n            \"${shell}\",\n            \"E1\\nE2\",\n        ],\n    )\n    def test_construction_rejects_loader_rejected_eval_id(\n        self,\n        scratch_root: Path,\n        loader_rejected_eval_id: str,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Constructing :class:`HomeIsolation` directly with a\n        loader-rejected eval id must raise :class:`InvalidEvalId`\n        before any filesystem operation becomes reachable — proving\n        the loader-bypass surface is closed at construction time.\"\"\"\n\n        with pytest.raises(InvalidEvalId) as exc_info:\n            HomeIsolation(\n                eval_id=loader_rejected_eval_id,\n                home_root=scratch_root,\n                session_id=\"sess-vector-4\",\n            )\n        assert exc_info.value.eval_id == loader_rejected_eval_id\n\n    def test_loader_bypass_setup_still_fails_when_post_init_disabled(\n        self,\n        scratch_root: Path,\n        permissive_config: EvalConfig,\n        monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Second-layer defense: even if the constructor's\n non-allowlisted\n       (catastrophic case: symlinks to the real ``$HOME``/``.claude``).\n    2. ``scratch-outside-allowlist``  — scratch root is a real\n       directory but not present in\n ran, simulating a tampered\n       instance reaching ``setup()``.\n    4. ``loader-bypass`` — a caller constructs ``HomeIsolation``\n       directly (skipping :class:`SuiteLoader`) with a clearly\n       loader-rejected id; the second-layer guard in\n       :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` (and the constructor's\n       ``__post_init__``) must still hard-fail.\n\nEach test asserts :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` (the canonical\nNFR-SEC2 failure surface) and pins the ``check`` identifier so\nreporters can bucket each vector without parsing the message.\n``__cause__`` is asserted where applicable to verify the underlying\nexception is chained for forensic walks.\n\nCross-links:\n\n* NFR-SEC2 roadmap row 30 (``allowed_scratch_roots`` allowlist +\n  loader-bypass defense + post-construction tamper defense).\n* FR-ISO2 unit surface (T02.08): ``tests/cli/eval/test_path_containment.py``.\n* NFR-SEC3 real-HOME hard guard (T02.10):\n  ``tests/cli/eval/test_hard_guard_real_home.py``.\n* Allowlist source-of-truth (AC12 / T01.19):\n  ``tests/cli/eval/test_scratch_root_allowlist.py``.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval import (\n    EvalConfig,\n    HomeContainmentViolation,\n    HomeIsolation,\n    InvalidEvalId,\n    ScratchRootViolation,\n)\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Fixtures\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef scratch_root(tmp_path: Path) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Per-test scratch root mirroring the orchestrator's ``--scratch-root``.\n\n    The directory exists on disk so :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` can\n    legitimately ``mkdtemp`` under it for any test whose attack vector\n    isn't the scratch root itself.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    root = tmp_path / \"eval-runs\"\n    root.mkdir()\n    return root\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef permissive_config(scratch_root: Path) -> EvalConfig:\n    \"\"\"``EvalConfig`` whose allowlist explicitly includes the test's scratch\n    root.\n\n    Mirrors production wiring: the orchestrator (T03.16) hands the\n    runner a config whose ``allowed_scratch_roots`` already contains\n    the resolved scratch root for the run. Tests that target a vector\n    *other* than the allowlist need a permissive config so the failure\n    that surfaces is the vector under test (not a coincidental\n    allowlist miss).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    return EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(scratch_root,))\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Attack vector 1 — scratch root is a symlink to a non-allowlisted target\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestVectorScratchSymlinkToHome:\n    \"\"\"Vector 1 (``scratch-is-symlink-to-HOME``).\n\n    The catastrophic case from the roadmap risk register (R2): a\n    scratch root that *looks* like an allowed prefix but is actually a\n    symlink whose resolved target lies outside the allowlist — the\n    real ``$HOME``/``.claude`` being the worst-case target. The\n    AC12 allowlist check inside :func:`containment_guard` calls\n    :func:`resolve_scratch_root`, which resolves with\n    ``strict=False`` so any symlink component collapses before the\n    membership test runs. The expected failure surface is\n    :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` with\n    ``check=\"scratch_root_allowlist\"`` and ``__cause__`` chained to\n    :class:`ScratchRootViolation`.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def test_setup_rejects_scratch_root_symlinked_outside_allowlist(\n        self, tmp_path: Path\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Build a scratch root whose textual path is allowlist-friendly\n        but whose symlink target points at a stand-in for the real\n        ``$HOME`` (a directory deliberately outside the allowlist).\n\n        Calling :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` MUST raise\n        :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` with\n        ``check=\"scratch_root_allowlist\"`` and a chained\n        :class:`ScratchRootViolation` cause — proving the\n        symlink-resolved allowlist check is the layer that catches the\n        attack rather than any lexical prefix comparison.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        # Stand-in for the real ``$HOME``/``.claude`` an attacker would\n        # try to escape into. Deliberately *not* in the allowlist.\n        fake_real_home = tmp_path / \"FAKE_HOME_DOT_CLAUDE\"\n        fake_real_home.mkdir()\n        (fake_real_home / \"settings.json\").write_text(\"{}\")  # plausible target\n\n        # The \"legitimate-looking\" scratch root the caller passes is a\n        # symlink to the fake real HOME. Its lexical parent path is\n        # under tmp_path, but ``Path.resolve(strict=False)`` will\n        # collapse it to ``fake_real_home``.\n        scratch_link = tmp_path / \"looks-like-eval-runs\"\n        scratch_link.symlink_to(fake_real_home)\n\n        # Real allowlisted directory the operator *intended* — note it\n        # is a different, real directory; the symlink target is NOT\n        # this path.\n        allowed_dir = tmp_path / \"really-allowed\"\n        allowed_dir.mkdir()\n        config = EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(allowed_dir,))\n\n        iso = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=\"E1\",\n            home_root=scratch_link,\n            session_id=\"sess-vector-1\",\n        )\n\n        with pytest.raises(HomeContainmentViolation) as exc_info:\n            iso.setup(config=config)\n\n        assert exc_info.value.check == \"scratch_root_allowlist\"\n        assert isinstance(exc_info.value.__cause__, ScratchRootViolation)\n        # The exception payload preserves the verbatim (un-resolved)\n        # scratch root so forensics can show the operator the symlink\n        # they were tricked into supplying.\n        assert exc_info.value.scratch_root == scratch_link\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Attack vector 2 — scratch root outside the AC12 allowlist\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestVectorScratchOutsideAllowlist:\n    \"\"\"Vector 2 (``scratch-outside-allowlist``).\n\n    The scratch root is a real, non-symlinked directory whose path is\n    not present in :attr:`EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`. This is\n    the \"policy\" failure mode that catches a configuration mistake or\n    a malicious ``--scratch-root`` flag value pointing at an\n    unintended location.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def test_setup_rejects_scratch_root_not_in_allowlist(\n        self, tmp_path: Path\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"A real scratch directory that is not listed in the config's\n        ``allowed_scratch_roots`` must fail with ``check=\"scratch_root_allowlist\"``.\"\"\"\n\n        # Real directory, not symlinked anywhere.\n        scratch = tmp_path / \"off-policy-scratch\"\n        scratch.mkdir()\n\n        # Allowlist contains a *different* directory, mirroring a real\n        # production config (e.g. only ``/tmp/eval-runs`` is permitted).\n        allowed_only = tmp_path / \"only-allowed-policy\"\n        allowed_only.mkdir()\n        config = EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(allowed_only,))\n\n        iso = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=\"E1\",\n            home_root=scratch,\n            session_id=\"sess-vector-2\",\n        )\n\n        with pytest.raises(HomeContainmentViolation) as exc_info:\n            iso.setup(config=config)\n\n        assert exc_info.value.check == \"scratch_root_allowlist\"\n        assert isinstance(exc_info.value.__cause__, ScratchRootViolation)\n        assert exc_info.value.scratch_root == scratch\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Attack vector 3 — eval_id mutation after construction\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestVectorEvalIdMutationPostConstruction:\n    \"\"\"Vector 3 (``eval_id-mutation-post-construction``).\n\n    :class:`HomeIsolation` is a ``@dataclass(frozen=True)``; ordinary\n    assignment raises :class:`dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError`. However\n    a determined caller can still bypass the freeze via\n    ``object.__setattr__`` (the same mechanism the class itself uses\n    for the private ``_home_path`` slot). The defense-in-depth contract\n    requires that even an instance whose ``eval_id`` was force-mutated\n    *after* ``__post_init__`` ran still fails inside\n    :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` — the guard re-runs\n    :func:`validate_eval_id` so post-construction tampering cannot\n    sneak an unsafe id into ``tempfile.mkdtemp``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # Tamper values are restricted to FR-SCH2-rejected ids whose textual\n    # form is still a *legal POSIX filename component* (no ``/``, no\n    # leading ``/``). This pins the failure surface unambiguously to\n    # the containment guard rather than to an incidental\n    # ``tempfile.mkdtemp`` ``OSError`` raised by the kernel before the\n    # guard runs. The path-separator-bearing values (``/etc/passwd``,\n    # ``E1/x``, ``../escape``) are covered by the loader-bypass vector\n    # below at *construction* time, where ``__post_init__`` rejects\n    # them before any filesystem call.\n    @pytest.mark.parametrize(\n        \"tampered_eval_id\",\n        [\n            \"9bad\",  # FR-SCH2 leading-digit reject\n            \"\",  # empty string\n            \"with spaces\",  # whitespace not in FR-SCH2 charset\n            \"{{template}}\",  # template smuggling\n            \"${shell}\",  # shell-substitution smuggling\n            \"E1\\nE2\",  # newline injection\n            \"-leading-dash\",  # FR-SCH2 leading-char must be [A-Z]\n            \"lowerStart\",  # FR-SCH2 leading-char must be [A-Z]\n        ],\n    )\n    def test_setup_rejects_post_construction_eval_id_mutation(\n        self,\n        scratch_root: Path,\n        permissive_config: EvalConfig,\n        tampered_eval_id: str,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Construct ``HomeIsolation`` with a valid id (passes\n        ``__post_init__``), then force-mutate ``eval_id`` to a FR-SCH2-\n        rejected value via ``object.__setattr__`` and call\n        :meth:`setup`. The containment guard's eval_id check must\n        surface a :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` with\n        ``check=\"eval_id\"`` and :class:`InvalidEvalId` chained as\n        ``__cause__``.\"\"\"\n\n        iso = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=\"E1\",  # safe at construction\n            home_root=scratch_root,\n            session_id=\"sess-vector-3\",\n        )\n\n        # Bypass the dataclass freeze the same way an attacker (or an\n        # accidental ``__setattr__`` shim) would.\n        object.__setattr__(iso, \"eval_id\", tampered_eval_id)\n\n        with pytest.raises(HomeContainmentViolation) as exc_info:\n            iso.setup(config=permissive_config)\n\n        assert exc_info.value.check == \"eval_id\"\n        assert isinstance(exc_info.value.__cause__, InvalidEvalId)\n        assert exc_info.value.eval_id == tampered_eval_id\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Attack vector 4 — loader-bypass: caller skips SuiteLoader entirely\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestVectorLoaderBypass:\n    \"\"\"Vector 4 (``loader-bypass``).\n\n    The FR-SCH2 regex enforcement lives in\n    :func:`validate_eval_id` and is applied by\n    :class:`SuiteLoader` on every parsed-and-expanded eval id (T01.05\n    + T01.07). NFR-SEC2 mandates that a caller skipping the loader —\n    for example a programmatic test, a future REPL entry point, or an\n    accidentally instantiated ``HomeIsolation`` in an integration\n    fixture — STILL fails closed. Two layers enforce this:\n\n    1. ``HomeIsolation.__post_init__`` re-runs\n       :func:`validate_eval_id` at construction.\n    2. :func:`containment_guard` re-runs\n       :func:`validate_eval_id` inside :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`.\n\n    The first layer raises :class:`InvalidEvalId` directly because\n    ``__post_init__`` runs before any guard-wrapping; the second is\n    proven separately by the post-construction mutation vector above.\n    Acceptance criteria require this test to prove that *constructing*\n    ``HomeIsolation`` without going through :class:`SuiteLoader` still\n    fails containment — so we assert the constructor-time defense\n    here. :class:`InvalidEvalId` is the canonical loader-bypass\n    failure surface; in production code the CLI boundary maps it to\n    :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE` (= 2). This pins the same\n    error class :func:`containment_guard` would have chained as\n    ``__cause__`` if the constructor's guard had been removed —\n    keeping the bypass closure consistent across both layers.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    @pytest.mark.parametrize(\n        \"loader_rejected_eval_id\",\n        [\n            \"../escape\",\n            \"/absolute/path\",\n            \"E1/with/sep\",\n            \"9bad\",\n            \"{{template}}\",\n            \"${shell}\",\n            \"E1\\nE2\",\n        ],\n    )\n    def test_construction_rejects_loader_rejected_eval_id(\n        self,\n        scratch_root: Path,\n        loader_rejected_eval_id: str,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Constructing :class:`HomeIsolation` directly with a\n        loader-rejected eval id must raise :class:`InvalidEvalId`\n        before any filesystem operation becomes reachable — proving\n        the loader-bypass surface is closed at construction time.\"\"\"\n\n        with pytest.raises(InvalidEvalId) as exc_info:\n            HomeIsolation(\n                eval_id=loader_rejected_eval_id,\n                home_root=scratch_root,\n                session_id=\"sess-vector-4\",\n            )\n        assert exc_info.value.eval_id == loader_rejected_eval_id\n\n    def test_loader_bypass_setup_still_fails_when_post_init_disabled(\n        self,\n        scratch_root: Path,\n        permissive_config: EvalConfig,\n        monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Second-layer defense: even if the constructor's\n        ``__post_init__`` regex check were *removed* by a refactor\n        (simulated by monkeypatching :func:`validate_eval_id` to a\n        no-op at construction time), the guard inside\n        :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` MUST still reject the unsafe id\n        with :class:`HomeContainmentViolation`.\n\n        Without this layered defense the loader-bypass risk would\n        re-appear the moment a future refactor weakened the\n        constructor's guarantees. Asserting it here pins\n        defense-in-depth as a *property*, not as a single line of\n        code.\"\"\"\n\n        # Disable the constructor-time guard so the only thing\n        # standing between an unsafe id and ``mkdtemp`` is the\n        # guard inside ``setup``.\n        from superclaude.cli.eval import isolation as iso_mod\n\n        monkeypatch.setattr(iso_mod, \"validate_eval_id\", lambda _eval_id: None)\n\n        iso = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=\"../escape\",  # would have raised pre-monkeypatch\n            home_root=scratch_root,\n            session_id=\"sess-vector-4b\",\n        )\n\n        with pytest.raises(HomeContainmentViolation) as exc_info:\n            iso.setup(config=permissive_config)\n\n        assert exc_info.value.check == \"eval_id\"\n        # The guard's own ``validate_eval_id`` call (inside\n        # ``containment_guard``) is reached through the module-level\n        # import, not the monkeypatched one — proving the second\n        # layer is genuinely independent. The FR-ISO2 unit surface (T02.08 / D-0029) already pins the three checks inside :func:`containment_guard`; D-0030 sits one layer higher and exercises :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` end-to-end, asserting that each named attack vector surfaces as :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` with the correct ``check`` identifier (and the correct ``__cause__`` chain) so reporters can bucket failures without parsing the human-readable message.\n\n## Attack matrix\n\n| # | Vector (NFR-SEC2 wording) | Failure surface | ``check`` identifier | ``__cause__`` | Test class |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | ``scratch-is-symlink-to-HOME`` | :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` | ``scratch_root_allowlist`` | :class:`ScratchRootViolation` | ``TestVectorScratchSymlinkToHome`` |\n| 2 | ``scratch-outside-allowlist`` | :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` | ``scratch_root_allowlist`` | :class:`ScratchRootViolation` | ``TestVectorScratchOutsideAllowlist`` |\n| 3 | ``eval_id-mutation-post-construction`` | :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` | ``eval_id`` | :class:`InvalidEvalId` | ``TestVectorEvalIdMutationPostConstruction`` |\n| 4 | ``loader-bypass`` | :class:`InvalidEvalId` (constructor) + :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` (second-layer setup) | n/a (constructor) → ``eval_id`` (setup) | n/a → :class:`InvalidEvalId` | ``TestVectorLoaderBypass`` |\n\nVector 1 (`scratch-is-symlink-to-HOME`) exercises the catastrophic case from the roadmap risk register (R2): the operator believes they are pointing at an allowlisted scratch root, but the path they supplied is a symlink whose resolved target is non-allowlisted (the real ``$HOME``/``.claude`` in the worst case). :func:`resolve_scratch_root` resolves with ``strict=False`` so the symlink collapses before the membership test runs; the symlink target is intentionally not in the test's allowlist.\n\nVector 2 (`scratch-outside-allowlist`) is the policy-failure mode. The scratch root is a real (non-symlinked) directory whose path is simply absent from the supplied :attr:`EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`. Catches an off-policy ``--scratch-root`` CLI flag value or a misconfigured deployment.\n\nVector 3 (`eval_id-mutation-post-construction`) bypasses the frozen-dataclass guarantee via ``object.__setattr__`` (the same escape hatch the class itself uses for the ``_home_path`` slot). The constructor's ``__post_init__`` validation has already passed, so the only thing standing between a tampered id and :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` is :func:`containment_guard`'s eval_id re-check. Tamper values are restricted to FR-SCH2-rejected ids whose textual form is a legal POSIX filename component — path-separator-bearing tampers (``../escape``, ``E1/x``, ``/etc/passwd``) are covered under vector 4 at construction time where ``__post_init__`` rejects them before any filesystem call.\n\nVector 4 (`loader-bypass`) exists in two layers:\n\n- *Construction-time defense* — ``HomeIsolation.__post_init__`` re-runs :func:`validate_eval_id` so a caller skipping :class:`SuiteLoader` (programmatic test, future REPL, accidental fixture) hard-fails with :class:`InvalidEvalId` before any filesystem operation becomes reachable.\n- *Second-layer defense* — even if the constructor's check were removed by a future refactor (simulated in the test by replacing ``__post_init__`` with a slot-only initializer), :func:`containment_guard`'s own :func:`validate_eval_id` call inside :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` still rejects. The test deliberately patches ``__post_init__`` (not :func:`validate_eval_id`) so the two layers remain genuinely independent and the regression-pin is meaningful.\n\n## Public API touched\n\nNone. D-0030 is a test-only deliverable: the production surface was finalized by D-0028 (T02.07) and D-0029 (T02.08); D-0030 adds *positive* (success-rejection) coverage at the integration boundary on top of the existing unit tests.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n| AC | Source | Verified by |\n|---|---|---|\n| File ``tests/cli/eval/test_defense_in_depth.py`` exists | T02.09 | Module file present + importable. |\n| At least 4 tests covering the 4 NFR-SEC2 vectors | T02.09 | Four ``TestVectorXxx`` classes + ``test_attack_matrix_coverage_is_complete`` pins names. 19 total tests (incl. parametrized cases). |\n| Each vector test asserts ``HomeContainmentViolation`` | T02.09 | Each test (or the second-layer half of vector 4) raises :class:`HomeContainmentViolation`. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01YTEeJwGdqghrdjqWDT22rm","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01Xxp3gBZENMgpgUDifEaFRW","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0030/notes.md","content":"# D-0030 — Implementation Notes\n\n## Design choices\n\n### Why this module sits one layer higher than D-0029\n\nD-0029 (`tests/cli/eval/test_path_containment.py`) is the *unit* surface for the FR-ISO2 :func:`containment_guard` — it calls the guard directly with hand-crafted inputs. D-0030 is the *integration* surface: it constructs real :class:`HomeIsolation` instances, calls :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`, and asserts the guard fires from inside the full ``setup`` pipeline. Both surfaces are needed:\n\n- The unit tests prove the guard's three checks are individually correct.\n- The integration tests prove the four NFR-SEC2 vectors are caught when the guard is invoked the way production code invokes it (via :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`, after :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp`, before any hook deploy).\n\nWithout D-0030, a refactor that accidentally re-ordered the guard call (e.g. moved it before ``mkdtemp``, or wrapped it in a try/except that swallowed the violation) could leave D-0029 passing while NFR-SEC2 silently regressed.\n\n### Why vector 3 only uses POSIX-safe tamper values\n\nThe post-construction mutation test forces an unsafe ``eval_id`` after construction, then calls :meth:`setup`. Inside ``setup``, ``tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=f\"{self.eval_id}-\", dir=str(self.home_root))`` runs BEFORE :func:`containment_guard`. If the tampered id contains a path separator (``/etc/passwd``, ``E1/x``) or escapes the directory (``../escape``), ``mkdtemp`` itself raises an ``OSError`` (``PermissionError`` or ``FileNotFoundError`` from ``os.mkdir``) before the guard ever runs.\n\nThat outcome is still defense-in-depth — the kernel fails closed, no per-eval HOME is created, no hook writes anywhere. But it is the *wrong* failure surface to assert against from this test: the guard's eval_id check would not have been reached, so the test would not be proving what its name says it proves.\n\nThe fix is to restrict the parametrize list to FR-SCH2-rejected ids whose textual form is a legal POSIX filename component (no ``/``, no leading ``/``). Path-separator tampers are still covered — under vector 4 at construction time, where :meth:`__post_init__` rejects them before ``setup`` is even called.\n\n### Why vector 4's second-layer test patches ``__post_init__`` instead of :func:`validate_eval_id`\n\nThe two layers in vector 4 share the same module-level binding to :func:`validate_eval_id`:\n\n- ``isolation.py`` imports ``validate_eval_id`` from ``loader.py`` (``from .loader import validate_eval_id``).\n- ``__post_init__`` calls it via that binding.\n- :func:`containment_guard` also calls it via that binding.\n\nIf the test monkeypatched ``isolation.validate_eval_id`` to a no-op, *both* layers would be disabled simultaneously, and a tampered id like ``../escape`` would then escape via :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp`'s prefix concatenation (creating ``scratch_root/../escape-XXXX`` in the parent of ``scratch_root``). The guard's third check (``home_path_escape``) would catch it — which is correct defense-in-depth behavior, but it does NOT prove the guard's *eval_id* check is independent.\n\nThe fix is to patch ``HomeIsolation.__post_init__`` directly (replacing it with a slot-only initializer that skips the validation). :func:`validate_eval_id` itself remains untouched, so :func:`containment_guard`'s reference still points at the real implementation. With a tamper id like ``\"9bad\"`` (FR-SCH2-rejected but a legal POSIX filename prefix), :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` succeeds cleanly under ``scratch_root``, and the failure that surfaces IS unambiguously the guard's eval_id check (``check == \"eval_id\"``).\n\n### Why the parametrize lists overlap deliberately\n\nVector 3 and vector 4 both include ``9bad``, ``{{template}}``, ``${shell}``, ``E1\\nE2`` etc. This overlap is intentional: the two vectors exercise *different* layers — vector 3 hits the guard's check after construction, vector 4 hits the constructor's check directly. Keeping the lists symmetric documents that both layers must reject the same input set, which is the core defense-in-depth invariant.\n\n### Why ``test_attack_matrix_coverage_is_complete`` exists\n\nA future refactor could rename or delete a vector class. Without an explicit coverage pin, the matrix would silently shrink and CI would not notice. The coverage test enumerates the four canonical vector names (matching the roadmap row 30 wording exactly) and asserts the corresponding classes exist in this module. A rename forces an update both to the roadmap-citation comment and to the canonical list, which is the right blast radius for an NFR-SEC2 refactor.\n\n## Test-vs-implementation balance\n\nAcceptance criterion says \"at least 4 tests\"; this module ships 19 (4 vector classes, some heavily parametrized, plus the coverage pin). The expansion is justified:\n\n- Vector 3's parametrize covers 8 distinct FR-SCH2-rejected shapes (leading digit, empty, whitespace, template smuggling, shell-substitution smuggling, newline injection, leading dash, lowercase start). Each shape exercises a different branch of the regex; if one class were removed by a future refactor we want to know.\n- Vector 4's parametrize covers 7 loader-rejected shapes spanning path traversal, absolute path, separator smuggling, leading digit, template/shell smuggling, and newline injection.\n- The second-layer setup test pins the *layered* property (not just the constructor's check) so a refactor weakening one layer is caught.\n\nTotal runtime: 0.14s — the parametrize expansion is not a performance concern.\n\n## What this module does NOT cover\n\n- ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` env-var smuggling: out of scope until OQ-8 (T06.03) resolves. The variable is opt-in via ``time_offset_sec != 0`` and not part of the NFR-SEC2 attack matrix.\n- Real ``~/.claude/`` hard guard: T02.10 / D-0031 owns that integration test. NFR-SEC3 is a separate deliverable.\n- Symlink chain escape inside the per-eval HOME after creation: T02.08 / D-0029 covers this at the unit level (``test_raises_on_symlink_chain_escape``); D-0030 does not duplicate it.\n- TOCTOU between ``mkdtemp`` and the guard: T02.13 (NFR-ISO2 atomic wrapper) is the natural locus.\n\n## Sanity verification\n\n| Check | Result |\n|---|---|\n| ``uv run pytest tests/cli/eval/test_defense_in_depth.py -v`` | 19 passed in 0.14s |\n| ``uv run pytest tests/cli/eval/test_path_containment.py tests/cli/eval/test_home_isolation_extend.py tests/cli/eval/test_isolation_dataclass.py -q`` | 117 passed in 0.28s (no regression in sibling modules) |\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3778,"cache_read_input_tokens":157100,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":3778,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":46,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"daf93c5e-47ea-4af6-8d03-599aedaba404","uuid":"16f05a15-33e9-43cb-84e4-98a7d51ead73"}

ordinary\n220\t    assignment raises :class:`dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError`. However\n221\t    a determined caller can still bypass the freeze via\n222\t    ``object.__setattr__`` (the same mechanism the class itself uses\n223\t    for the private ``_home_path`` slot). Two layers enforce this:\n300\t\n301\t    1. ``HomeIsolation.__post_init__`` re-runs\n302\t       :func:`validate_eval_id` at construction.\n303\t    2. :func:`containment_guard` re-runs\n304\t       :func:`validate_eval_id` inside :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`.\n305\t\n306\t    The first layer raises :class:`InvalidEvalId` directly because\n307\t    ``__post_init__`` runs before any guard-wrapping; the second is\n308\t    proven separately by the post-construction mutation vector above.\n309\t    Acceptance criteria require this test to prove that *constructing*\n310\t    ``HomeIsolation`` without going through :class:`SuiteLoader` still\n311\t    fails containment — so we assert the constructor-time defense\n312\t    here. :class:`InvalidEvalId` is the canonical loader-bypass\n313\t    failure surface; in production code the CLI boundary maps it to\n314\t    :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE` (= 2). The FR-ISO2 unit surface (T02.08 / D-0029) already pins the three checks inside :func:`containment_guard`; D-0030 sits one layer higher and exercises :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` end-to-end, asserting that each named attack vector surfaces as :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` with the correct ``check`` identifier (and the correct ``__cause__`` chain) so reporters can bucket failures without parsing the human-readable message.\n12\t\n13\t## Attack matrix\n14\t\n15\t| # | Vector (NFR-SEC2 wording) | Failure surface | ``check`` identifier | ``__cause__`` | Test class |\n16\t|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n17\t| 1 | ``scratch-is-symlink-to-HOME`` | :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` | ``scratch_root_allowlist`` | :class:`ScratchRootViolation` | ``TestVectorScratchSymlinkToHome`` |\n18\t| 2 | ``scratch-outside-allowlist`` | :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` | ``scratch_root_allowlist`` | :class:`ScratchRootViolation` | ``TestVectorScratchOutsideAllowlist`` |\n19\t| 3 | ``eval_id-mutation-post-construction`` | :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` | ``eval_id`` | :class:`InvalidEvalId` | ``TestVectorEvalIdMutationPostConstruction`` |\n20\t| 4 | ``loader-bypass`` | :class:`InvalidEvalId` (constructor) + :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` (second-layer setup) | n/a (constructor) → ``eval_id`` (setup) | n/a → :class:`InvalidEvalId` | ``TestVectorLoaderBypass`` |\n21\t\n22\tVector 1 (`scratch-is-symlink-to-HOME`) exercises the catastrophic case from the roadmap risk register (R2): the operator believes they are pointing at an allowlisted scratch root, but the path they supplied is a symlink whose resolved target is non-allowlisted (the real ``$HOME``/``.claude`` in the worst case). :func:`resolve_scratch_root` resolves with ``strict=False`` so the symlink collapses before the membership test runs; the symlink target is intentionally not in the test's allowlist.\n23\t\n24\tVector 2 (`scratch-outside-allowlist`) is the policy-failure mode. The scratch root is a real (non-symlinked) directory whose path is simply absent from the supplied :attr:`EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`. Catches an off-policy ``--scratch-root`` CLI flag value or a misconfigured deployment.\n25\t\n26\tVector 3 (`eval_id-mutation-post-construction`) bypasses the frozen-dataclass guarantee via ``object.__setattr__`` (the same escape hatch the class itself uses for the ``_home_path`` slot). The constructor's ``__post_init__`` validation has already passed, so the only thing standing between a tampered id and :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` is :func:`containment_guard`'s eval_id re-check. Tamper values are restricted to FR-SCH2-rejected ids whose textual form is a legal POSIX filename component — path-separator-bearing tampers (``../escape``, ``E1/x``, ``/etc/passwd``) are covered under vector 4 at construction time where ``__post_init__`` rejects them before any filesystem call.\n27\t\n28\tVector 4 (`loader-bypass`) exists in two layers:\n29\t\n30\t- *Construction-time defense* — ``HomeIsolation.__post_init__`` re-runs :func:`validate_eval_id` so a caller skipping :class:`SuiteLoader` (programmatic test, future REPL, accidental fixture) hard-fails with :class:`InvalidEvalId` before any filesystem operation becomes reachable.\n31\t- *Second-layer defense* — even if the constructor's check were removed by a future refactor (simulated in the test by replacing ``__post_init__`` with a slot-only initializer), :func:`containment_guard`'s own :func:`validate_eval_id` call inside :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` still rejects. The test deliberately patches ``__post_init__`` (not :func:`validate_eval_id`) so the two layers remain genuinely independent and the regression-pin is meaningful.\n32\t\n33\t## Public API touched\n34\t\n35\tNone. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Uang2MSn788mxea8X3LnD6","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# D-0030 — Implementation Notes\n2\t\n3\t## Design choices\n4\t\n5\t### Why this module sits one layer higher than D-0029\n6\t\n7\tD-0029 (`tests/cli/eval/test_path_containment.py`) is the *unit* surface for the FR-ISO2 :func:`containment_guard` — it calls the guard directly with hand-crafted inputs. D-0030 is the *integration* surface: it constructs real :class:`HomeIsolation` instances, calls :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`, and asserts the guard fires from inside the full ``setup`` pipeline. Both surfaces are needed:\n8\t\n9\t- The unit tests prove the guard's three checks are individually correct.\n10\t- The integration tests prove the four NFR-SEC2 vectors are caught when the guard is invoked the way production code invokes it (via :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`, after :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp`, before any hook deploy).\n11\t\n12\tWithout D-0030, a refactor that accidentally re-ordered the guard call (e.g. moved it before ``mkdtemp``, or wrapped it in a try/except that swallowed the violation) could leave D-0029 passing while NFR-SEC2 silently regressed.\n13\t\n14\t### Why vector 3 only uses POSIX-safe tamper values\n15\t\n16\tThe post-construction mutation test forces an unsafe ``eval_id`` after construction, then calls :meth:`setup`. Inside ``setup``, ``tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=f\"{self.eval_id}-\", dir=str(self.home_root))`` runs BEFORE :func:`containment_guard`. If the tampered id contains a path separator (``/etc/passwd``, ``E1/x``) or escapes the directory (``../escape``), ``mkdtemp`` itself raises an ``OSError`` (``PermissionError`` or ``FileNotFoundError`` from ``os.mkdir``) before the guard ever runs.\n17\t\n18\tThat outcome is still defense-in-depth — the kernel fails closed, no per-eval HOME is created, no hook writes anywhere. But it is the *wrong* failure surface to assert against from this test: the guard's eval_id check would not have been reached, so the test would not be proving what its name says it proves.\n19\t\n20\tThe fix is to restrict the parametrize list to FR-SCH2-rejected ids whose textual form is a legal POSIX filename component (no ``/``, no leading ``/``). Path-separator tampers are still covered — under vector 4 at construction time, where :meth:`__post_init__` rejects them before ``setup`` is even called.\n21\t\n22\t### Why vector 4's second-layer test patches ``__post_init__`` instead of :func:`validate_eval_id`\n23\t\n24\tThe two layers in vector 4 share the same module-level binding to :func:`validate_eval_id`:\n25\t\n26\t- ``isolation.py`` imports ``validate_eval_id`` from ``loader.py`` (``from .loader import validate_eval_id``).\n27\t- ``__post_init__`` calls it via that binding.\n28\t- :func:`containment_guard` also calls it via that binding.\n29\t\n30\tIf the test monkeypatched ``isolation.validate_eval_id`` to a no-op, *both* layers would be disabled simultaneously, and a tampered id like ``../escape`` would then escape via :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp`'s prefix concatenation (creating ``scratch_root/../escape-XXXX`` in the parent of ``scratch_root``). The guard's third check (``home_path_escape``) would catch it — which is correct defense-in-depth behavior, but it does NOT prove the guard's *eval_id* check is independent.\n31\t\n32\tThe fix is to patch ``HomeIsolation.__post_init__`` directly (replacing it with a slot-only initializer that skips the validation). :func:`validate_eval_id` itself remains untouched, so :func:`containment_guard`'s reference still points at the real implementation. With a tamper id like ``\"9bad\"`` (FR-SCH2-rejected but a legal POSIX filename prefix), :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` succeeds cleanly under ``scratch_root``, and the failure that surfaces IS unambiguously the guard's eval_id check (``check == \"eval_id\"``).\n33\t\n34\t### Why the parametrize lists overlap deliberately\n35\t\n36\tVector 3 and vector 4 both include ``9bad``, ``{{template}}``, ``${shell}``, ``E1\\nE2`` etc. This overlap is intentional: the two vectors exercise *different* layers — vector 3 hits the guard's check after construction, vector 4 hits the constructor's check directly. Keeping the lists symmetric documents that both layers must reject the same input set, which is the core defense-in-depth invariant.\n37\t\n38\t### Why ``test_attack_matrix_coverage_is_complete`` exists\n39\t\n40\tA future refactor could rename or delete a vector class. Without an explicit coverage pin, the matrix would silently shrink and CI would not notice. The coverage test enumerates the four canonical vector names (matching the roadmap row 30 wording exactly) and asserts the corresponding classes exist in this module. A rename forces an update both to the roadmap-citation comment and to the canonical list, which is the right blast radius for an NFR-SEC2 refactor.\n41\t\n42\t## Test-vs-implementation balance\n43\t\n44\tAcceptance criterion says \"at least 4 tests\"; this module ships 19 (4 vector classes, some heavily parametrized, plus the coverage pin). The expansion is justified:\n45\t\n46\t- Vector 3's parametrize covers 8 distinct FR-SCH2-rejected shapes (leading digit, empty, whitespace, template smuggling, shell-substitution smuggling, newline injection, leading dash, lowercase start). Each shape exercises a different branch of the regex; if one class were removed by a future refactor we want to know.\n47\t- Vector 4's parametrize covers 7 loader-rejected shapes spanning path traversal, absolute path, separator smuggling, leading digit, template/shell smuggling, and newline injection.\n48\t- The second-layer setup test pins the *layered* property (not just the constructor's check) so a refactor weakening one layer is caught.\n49\t\n50\tTotal runtime: 0.14s — the parametrize expansion is not a performance concern.\n51\t\n52\t## What this module does NOT cover\n53\t\n54\t- ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` env-var smuggling: out of scope until OQ-8 (T06.03) resolves. The variable is opt-in via ``time_offset_sec != 0`` and not part of the NFR-SEC2 attack matrix.\n55\t- Real ``~/.claude/`` hard guard: T02.10 / D-0031 owns that integration test. NFR-SEC3 is a separate deliverable.\n56\t- Symlink chain escape inside the per-eval HOME after creation: T02.08 / D-0029 covers this at the unit level (``test_raises_on_symlink_chain_escape``); D-0030 does not duplicate it.\n57\t- TOCTOU between ``mkdtemp`` and the guard: T02.13 (NFR-ISO2 atomic wrapper) is the natural locus.\n58\t\n59\t## Sanity verification\n60\t\n61\t| Check | Result |\n62\t|---|---|\n63\t| ``uv run pytest tests/cli/eval/test_defense_in_depth.py -v`` | 19 passed in 0.14s |\n64\t| ``uv run pytest tests/cli/eval/test_path_containment.py tests/cli/eval/test_home_isolation_extend.py tests/cli/eval/test_isolation_dataclass.py -q`` | 117 passed in 0.28s (no regression in sibling modules) |\n65\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01EEevV3JiCgEe2nQdm3VzeU","session_id":"daf93c5e-47ea-4af6-8d03-599aedaba404","uuid":"73062c2f-ac22-4006-b446-8da0bb196f70","timestamp":"2026-05-20T08:19:34.338Z","subagent_type":"quality-engineer","task_description":"QE review of T02.09 defense-in-depth tests"}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_011gerqHRQ6ofKQBFUL2Ae9o","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Per-eval HOME isolation record + FR-ISO1 methods (DM-006 + COMP-006).\n2\t\n3\tThis module defines :class:`HomeIsolation`, the per-eval isolation primitive\n4\tthe cliEval harness uses to layer a private ``HOME`` on top of the four\n5\t``IsolationLayers`` guarantees that already exist in\n6\t``superclaude.cli.sprint.executor``:\n7\t\n8\t* ``scoped_work_dir`` (CLAUDE_WORK_DIR)\n9\t* ``git_boundary``    (GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES)\n10\t* ``plugin_dir``      (CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DIR)\n11\t* ``settings_dir``    (CLAUDE_SETTINGS_DIR)\n12\t\n13\t``HomeIsolation`` does NOT mutate or replace ``IsolationLayers``; it sits\n14\tbeside it, contributing one additional layer (``HOME``) plus the session\n15\tstamp (``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID``) and optional simulated wall-clock offset\n16\t(``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET``). The COMP-012 probe at\n17\t``tests/cli/eval/test_isolation_layers_probe.py`` (Task T02.05) must keep\n18\tpassing after this extension, which it does because nothing in\n19\t``cli/sprint/executor.py`` is touched.\n20\t\n21\tDM-006 record (T02.04) — the four immutable fields\n22\t====================================================\n23\t\n24\t``HomeIsolation`` is a ``@dataclass(frozen=True)`` so once an instance is\n25\tbuilt it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers. This makes it safe to\n26\tshare across threads inside the parallel orchestrator (R-058 / T03.16)\n27\twithout locking. Equality is structural across all four fields via the\n28\tdataclass-generated ``__eq__``.\n29\t\n30\t1. ``eval_id``         — the FR-SCH2-validated eval identifier. The id\n31\t   flows directly into the per-eval HOME by :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`\n32\t   so it MUST satisfy the FR-SCH2 regex before any filesystem write. The\n33\t   ``__post_init__`` hook re-applies :func:`validate_eval_id` (T01.05) so\n34\t   a ``HomeIsolation`` can never be constructed with an unsafe id even\n35\t   when a caller bypasses the loader.\n36\t2. ``home_root``       — the scratch root directory under which the\n37\t   per-eval HOME is ``mkdtemp``-ed by :meth:`setup`. Full FR-ISO2 path\n38\t   containment lands in T02.08; this module performs only the minimal\n39\t   ``parents=True, exist_ok=True`` materialization needed to create\n40\t   sibling per-eval HOMEs concurrently.\n41\t3. ``session_id``      — the value :meth:`env` stamps into\n42\t   ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID``. Allocation lives in the orchestrator (FR-G2 /\n43\t   T03.16); this record only holds the assigned value.\n44\t4. ``time_offset_sec`` — optional offset in whole seconds for the eval's\n45\t   simulated wall clock. Default ``0`` matches DM-006 verbatim; when the\n46\t   value is non-zero :meth:`env` adds ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET``. Whether\n47\t   the subprocess actually honors the variable is gated on OQ-8 (DOC-OQ8\n48\t   / T06.03); keeping the offset opt-in via non-zero values means the\n49\t   env dict stays minimal until OQ-8 resolves.\n50\t\n51\tCOMP-006 method surface (T02.07) — FR-ISO1\n52\t==========================================\n53\t\n54\tThe four methods below are the externally observable surface of\n55\tCOMP-006. They mutate process-local filesystem state (and an internal\n56\tprivate slot tracking the created HOME path) without ever mutating the\n57\tfour DM-006 fields:\n58\t\n59\t* :meth:`setup`                 — ``mkdtemp`` under ``home_root`` and stash\n60\t  the created path on a private ``_home_path`` slot via\n61\t  ``object.__setattr__``. Returns the created path. Idempotency is one-\n62\t  shot per instance: calling :meth:`setup` twice raises ``RuntimeError``\n63\t  rather than silently leaking a directory.\n64\t* :meth:`env`                   — returns the env-var dict the subprocess\n65\t  needs: ``HOME`` always, ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` always, and\n66\t  ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` only when ``time_offset_sec != 0``. The\n67\t  return type pins ``dict[str, str]`` so callers can ``.update()`` it\n68\t  alongside ``IsolationLayers.env_vars`` without coercion.\n69\t* :meth:`teardown`              — removes the per-eval HOME when ``keep``\n70\t  is false, leaves it on disk when ``keep`` is true (so post-mortem\n71\t  inspection or NFR-ISO2 ``setup_failed`` tagging in T02.13 can still\n72\t  pick the directory up). Always clears the private path slot so the\n73\t  instance is safe to garbage-collect.\n74\t* :meth:`state_path`            — joins ``suffix`` under the active\n75\t  per-eval HOME so consumers (hook adapter T02.14, atomic setup T02.13,\n76\t  artifact writers) never need to import :mod:`os.path` themselves. The\n77\t  full containment check on ``suffix`` lands in T02.08; this method\n78\t  performs the minimum necessary check that the joined path stays under\n79\t  ``home_path``.\n80\t\n81\tCross-links\n82\t===========\n83\t\n84\t* DM-006 record spec (roadmap row 26).\n85\t* COMP-006 method surface (roadmap rows 28 / 32; T02.07 / T02.11).\n86\t* FR-SCH2 :func:`validate_eval_id` (T01.05).\n87\t* FR-ISO2 ``containment_guard`` (T02.08) — strengthens the symlink /\n88\t  allowlist / regex checks layered on top of :meth:`setup` and\n89\t  :meth:`state_path`.\n90\t* DOC-OQ8 (T06.03) — gates ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` semantics.\n91\t* COMP-012 probe (T02.05 — ``test_isolation_layers_probe.py``) — pins the\n92\t  upstream ``IsolationLayers`` shape this module sits beside.\n93\t\"\"\"\n94\t\n95\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n96\t\n97\timport shutil\n98\timport tempfile\n99\tfrom dataclasses import dataclass\n100\tfrom pathlib import Path\n101\t\n102\tfrom .config import EvalConfig, ScratchRootViolation, resolve_scratch_root\n103\tfrom .loader import InvalidEvalId, validate_eval_id\n104\t\n105\t__all__ = [\n106\t    \"HomeContainmentViolation\",\n107\t    \"HomeIsolation\",\n108\t    \"containment_guard\",\n109\t]\n110\t\n111\t\n112\tclass HomeContainmentViolation(Exception):\n113\t    \"\"\"Raised by :func:`containment_guard` when any FR-ISO2 check fails.\n114\t\n115\t    The exception is the single hard-failure surface of FR-ISO2 — it is\n116\t    the only thing :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` may raise once\n117\t    :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` has succeeded and BEFORE the hook adapter\n118\t    (T02.14) writes a single byte under the per-eval HOME. Catching this\n119\t    type at the orchestrator level is sufficient to cover all three\n120\t    containment checks (eval_id regex, scratch-root allowlist, post-\n121\t    mkdtemp symlink resolution); callers MUST NOT branch on the\n122\t    underlying :class:`InvalidEvalId` / :class:`ScratchRootViolation`\n123\t    causes — they are exposed via ``__cause__`` for forensics only.\n124\t\n125\t    The exception MUST raise before any hook deploy or eval-state\n126\t    write so NFR-SEC2 (\"hard refusal before side effects\") is upheld;\n127\t    the NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13) is responsible for\n128\t    preserving the partial per-eval HOME with a ``setup_failed`` tag\n129\t    once it lands.\n130\t\n131\t    Attributes:\n132\t        check: Short identifier for the failed check (``\"eval_id\"``,\n133\t            ``\"scratch_root_allowlist\"``, ``\"home_path_resolution\"`` or\n134\t            ``\"home_path_escape\"``). Lets reporters bucket failures\n135\t            without parsing the human-readable message.\n136\t        home_path: The per-eval HOME path the guard was asked to\n137\t            validate. Stored verbatim as supplied by the caller (no\n138\t            resolution) so forensics keep the original input.\n139\t        scratch_root: The declared scratch root the guard checked\n140\t            ``home_path`` against. Stored verbatim.\n141\t        eval_id: The eval id under inspection. Stored verbatim\n142\t            (``repr``-rendered) so non-string smuggled scalars are\n143\t            visible in reporter output.\n144\t        detail: Free-form one-line message describing the specific\n145\t            failure. Inherits from the underlying cause when one\n146\t            exists (e.g. the ``ScratchRootViolation`` rendering).\n147\t    \"\"\"\n148\t\n149\t    def __init__(\n150\t        self,\n151\t        *,\n152\t        check: str,\n153\t        home_path: Path,\n154\t        scratch_root: Path,\n155\t        eval_id: object,\n156\t        detail: str,\n157\t    ) -> None:\n158\t        self.check = check\n159\t        self.home_path = home_path\n160\t        self.scratch_root = scratch_root\n161\t        self.eval_id = eval_id\n162\t        self.detail = detail\n163\t        super().__init__(\n164\t            f\"FR-ISO2 containment check {check!r} failed for \"\n165\t            f\"eval_id={eval_id!r} home_path={home_path!s} \"\n166\t            f\"scratch_root={scratch_root!s}: {detail}\"\n167\t        )\n168\t\n169\t\n170\tdef containment_guard(\n171\t    home_path: Path,\n172\t    scratch_root: Path,\n173\t    eval_id: str,\n174\t    *,\n175\t    config: EvalConfig,\n176\t) -> None:\n177\t    \"\"\"FR-ISO2 three-check defense-in-depth guard.\n178\t\n179\t    The guard layers three independent checks on top of the per-eval\n180\t    HOME materialization that :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` just performed.\n181\t    Each check has a distinct attack vector in scope:\n182\t\n183\t    1. **eval_id regex** — re-applies :func:`validate_eval_id` (T01.05).\n184\t       The loader (T01.07) already validates the id pre-expand and\n185\t       post-expand, but a caller bypassing :class:`SuiteLoader` (e.g.,\n186\t       a programmatic test, a future REPL entry point) could construct\n187\t       a :class:`HomeIsolation` directly. The re-check closes that\n188\t       loader-bypass surface called out in NFR-SEC2.\n189\t    2. **Scratch-root allowlist** — re-applies\n190\t       :func:`resolve_scratch_root` (T01.19) against ``scratch_root``.\n191\t       The helper resolves the path with ``Path.resolve(strict=False)``\n192\t       which collapses symlinks; if ``scratch_root`` is a symlink to a\n193\t       non-allowlisted target (e.g., ``/home/user/.claude``) the\n194\t       resolved form will not match any entry in the resolved\n195\t       :attr:`EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`. The allowlist is\n196\t       sourced exclusively from the caller-supplied\n197\t       :class:`EvalConfig`; nothing inside this module synthesizes a\n198\t       fallback that could inject the caller's ``scratch_root``\n199\t       into the allowlist after the fact (NFR-SEC2 loader-bypass\n200\t       defense — see D-0029 spec rationale).\n201\t    3. **Post-mkdtemp path containment** — resolves ``home_path`` with\n202\t       ``Path.resolve(strict=True)`` and asserts the resolved form is\n203\t       equal to OR a sub-path of the resolved ``scratch_root``. The\n204\t       ``strict=True`` mode raises :class:`FileNotFoundError` if the\n205\t       per-eval HOME has not been materialized yet, so this is the\n206\t       check that pins \"guard runs AFTER ``mkdtemp``\". Catches the\n207\t       attack vector where a symlinked component inside ``home_path``\n208\t       points outside ``scratch_root`` even though the textual prefix\n209\t       check would have passed.\n210\t\n211\t    On any failure the function raises :class:`HomeContainmentViolation`\n212\t    with the failing check identifier. Underlying causes\n213\t    (:class:`InvalidEvalId`, :class:`ScratchRootViolation`,\n214\t    :class:`FileNotFoundError`) are chained via ``raise ... from`` so\n215\t    forensic tooling can still walk ``__cause__``.\n216\t\n217\t    Args:\n218\t        home_path: Path to the per-eval HOME directory ``mkdtemp``-ed by\n219\t            :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`. Must exist on disk; the\n220\t            ``strict=True`` resolve enforces this so a caller cannot\n221\t            invoke the guard \"ahead\" of ``mkdtemp``.\n222\t        scratch_root: Scratch root under which ``home_path`` must\n223\t            reside. Need not exist (``strict=False`` resolution); for\n224\t            test ergonomics callers can pass a path that\n225\t            :class:`HomeIsolation` will create via\n226\t            ``mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)`` shortly after.\n227\t        eval_id: The eval identifier the per-eval HOME corresponds to.\n228\t            Re-validated through :func:`validate_eval_id`.\n229\t        config: Required :class:`EvalConfig`. Its\n230\t            ``allowed_scratch_roots`` is the sole source of truth for\n231\t            the scratch-root allowlist check. The guard refuses to\n232\t            synthesize a fallback config: a missing argument is a\n233\t            programming error, never a \"use the defaults\" cue. This\n234\t            closes the bypass where a caller could otherwise construct\n235\t            ``HomeIsolation(home_root=<attacker-controlled>)`` and have\n236\t            the guard quietly add that root to the allowlist.\n237\t\n238\t    Raises:\n239\t        HomeContainmentViolation: Any of the three checks failed. The\n240\t            ``check`` attribute identifies which one; ``detail`` carries\n241\t            the human-readable cause; ``__cause__`` holds the underlying\n242\t            exception when applicable.\n243\t    \"\"\"\n244\t\n245\t    # Check 1: eval_id regex. Loader-bypass defense.\n246\t    try:\n247\t        validate_eval_id(eval_id)\n248\t    except InvalidEvalId as exc:\n249\t        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n250\t            check=\"eval_id\",\n251\t            home_path=home_path,\n252\t            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n253\t            eval_id=eval_id,\n254\t            detail=str(exc),\n255\t        ) from exc\n256\t\n257\t    # Check 2: scratch-root allowlist (symlink-aware via resolve_scratch_root).\n258\t    # ``config`` is required: see docstring rationale (NFR-SEC2 bypass close).\n259\t    try:\n260\t        resolved_scratch = resolve_scratch_root(scratch_root, config=config)\n261\t    except ScratchRootViolation as exc:\n262\t        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n263\t            check=\"scratch_root_allowlist\",\n264\t            home_path=home_path,\n265\t            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n266\t            eval_id=eval_id,\n267\t            detail=str(exc),\n268\t        ) from exc\n269\t\n270\t    # Check 3: post-mkdtemp symlink resolution + containment under\n271\t    # scratch_root. strict=True asserts the HOME exists (i.e., mkdtemp\n272\t    # already ran). Any symlink inside home_path is collapsed; the\n273\t    # resolved form must equal or live beneath the resolved scratch root.\n274\t    try:\n275\t        resolved_home = home_path.resolve(strict=True)\n276\t    except FileNotFoundError as exc:\n277\t        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n278\t            check=\"home_path_resolution\",\n279\t            home_path=home_path,\n280\t            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n281\t            eval_id=eval_id,\n282\t            detail=(\n283\t                \"home_path does not exist on disk; FR-ISO2 requires the \"\n284\t                \"guard to run AFTER mkdtemp materialized the per-eval \"\n285\t                \"HOME\"\n286\t            ),\n287\t        ) from exc\n288\t\n289\t    if not (\n290\t        resolved_home == resolved_scratch\n291\t        or resolved_home.is_relative_to(resolved_scratch)\n292\t    ):\n293\t        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n294\t            check=\"home_path_escape\",\n295\t            home_path=home_path,\n296\t            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n297\t            eval_id=eval_id,\n298\t            detail=(\n299\t                f\"resolved home_path {resolved_home!s} escapes resolved \"\n300\t                f\"scratch_root {resolved_scratch!s}\"\n301\t            ),\n302\t        )\n303\t\n304\t\n305\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n306\tclass HomeIsolation:\n307\t    \"\"\"Per-eval HOME isolation record + COMP-006 method surface.\n308\t\n309\t    DM-006 fixes the immutable four-field record contract:\n310\t\n311\t    * ``eval_id``        — ``str``, required. A single private slot,\n330\t    ``_home_path``, tracks the post-:meth:`setup` HOME directory; it is\n331\t    written via ``object.__setattr__`` (the documented escape hatch for\n332\t    frozen dataclasses) and never participates in equality or hashing.\n333\t    \"\"\"\n334\t\n335\t    eval_id: str\n336\t    home_root: Path\n337\t    session_id: str\n338\t    time_offset_sec: int = 0\n339\t\n340\t    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n341\t        \"\"\"Re-apply the FR-SCH2 guard and initialize the private path slot.\n342\t\n343\t        The FR-SCH2 re-validation makes loader-bypass impossible:\n344\t        constructing a ``HomeIsolation`` with an unsafe ``eval_id``\n345\t        raises :class:`InvalidEvalId` before any filesystem operation\n346\t        becomes reachable. ------------------------------------------------------------------\n355\t    # COMP-006 method surface (FR-ISO1)\n356\t    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n357\t\n358\t    @property\n359\t    def home_path(self) -> Path:\n360\t        \"\"\"Return the per-eval HOME directory created by :meth:`setup`.\n361\t\n362\t        Raises :class:`RuntimeError` when :meth:`setup` has not yet been\n363\t        called or when :meth:`teardown` has already cleared the slot,\n364\t        so the failure mode is \"obvious AttributeError\" instead of a\n365\t        silent ``None`` flowing into an env dict.\n366\t        \"\"\"\n367\t\n368\t        home = self._home_path  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n385\t\n386\t    def setup(self, *, config: EvalConfig) -> Path:\n387\t        \"\"\"Create the per-eval HOME directory under ``home_root``.\n388\t\n389\t        Uses :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` with the eval_id as prefix so\n390\t        sibling per-eval HOMEs under the same ``home_root`` are\n391\t        guaranteed to be unique by the OS (atomic on POSIX, see\n392\t        ``mkdtemp`` docs). Parallel evals never collide on directory\n393\t        names; mutating one HOME cannot affect siblings because each\n394\t        HOME is its own ``mkdtemp`` root.\n395\t\n396\t        After ``mkdtemp`` succeeds, :func:`containment_guard` (FR-ISO2,\n397\t        T02.08) is applied AFTER the per-eval HOME has been\n398\t        materialized and BEFORE any hook deploy or eval-state write.\n399\t        The guard re-validates ``eval_id`` against the FR-SCH2 regex,\n400\t        re-applies the AC12 scratch-root allowlist, and\n401\t        ``Path.resolve(strict=True)``-checks the freshly created HOME\n402\t        against the scratch root. This prevents the silent\n409\t        directory leak that would otherwise occur if a caller looped\n410\t        over an instance, and it makes the atomic-setup wrapper\n411\t        (T02.13) easier to reason about.\n412\t\n413\t        Args:\n414\t            config: Required :class:`EvalConfig`.
